~ '^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$' AND s_higgsfield_sec.id = uak.higgsfield_api_key_secret::uuid\nLEFT JOIN vault.decrypted_secrets s_apify ON uak.apify_token ~ Review the complete diff above. This contains all code changes in the PR.


OBJECTIVE:
Perform a security-focused code review to identify HIGH-CONFIDENCE security vulnerabilities that could have real exploitation potential. This is not a general code review - focus ONLY on security implications newly added by this PR. Do not comment on existing security concerns.

CRITICAL INSTRUCTIONS:
1. MINIMIZE FALSE POSITIVES: Only flag issues where you're >80% confident of actual exploitability
2. AVOID NOISE: Skip theoretical issues, style concerns, or low-impact findings
3. FOCUS ON IMPACT: Prioritize vulnerabilities that could lead to unauthorized access, data breaches, or system compromise
4. EXCLUSIONS: Do NOT report the following issue types:
   - Denial of Service (DOS) vulnerabilities, even if they allow service disruption
   - Secrets or sensitive data stored on disk (these are handled by other processes)
   - Rate limiting or resource exhaustion issues

SECURITY CATEGORIES TO EXAMINE:

**Input Validation Vulnerabilities:**
- SQL injection via unsanitized user input
- Command injection in system calls or subprocesses
- XXE injection in XML parsing
- Template injection in templating engines
- NoSQL injection in database queries
- Path traversal in file operations

**Authentication & Authorization Issues:**
- Authentication bypass logic
- Privilege escalation paths
- Session management flaws
- JWT token vulnerabilities
- Authorization logic bypasses

**Crypto & Secrets Management:**
- Hardcoded API keys, passwords, or tokens
- Weak cryptographic algorithms or implementations
- Improper key storage or management
- Cryptographic randomness issues
- Certificate validation bypasses

**Injection & Code Execution:**
- Remote code execution via deseralization
- Pickle injection in Python
- YAML deserialization vulnerabilities
- Eval injection in dynamic code execution
- XSS vulnerabilities in web applications (reflected, stored, DOM-based)

**Data Exposure:**
- Sensitive data logging or storage
- PII handling violations
- API endpoint data leakage
- Debug information exposure

Additional notes:
- Even if something is only exploitable from the local network, it can still be a HIGH severity issue

ANALYSIS METHODOLOGY:

Phase 1 - Repository Context Research (Use file search tools):
- Identify existing security frameworks and libraries in use
- Look for established secure coding patterns in the codebase
- Examine existing sanitization and validation patterns
- Understand the project's security model and threat model

Phase 2 - Comparative Analysis:
- Compare new code changes against existing security patterns
- Identify deviations from established secure practices
- Look for inconsistent security implementations
- Flag code that introduces new attack surfaces

Phase 3 - Vulnerability Assessment:
- Examine each modified file for security implications
- Trace data flow from user inputs to sensitive operations
- Look for privilege boundaries being crossed unsafely
- Identify injection points and unsafe deserialization

REQUIRED OUTPUT FORMAT:

You MUST output your findings in markdown. The markdown output should contain the file, line number, severity, category (e.g. `sql_injection` or `xss`), description, exploit scenario, and fix recommendation.

For example:

# Vuln 1: XSS: `foo.py:42`

* Severity: High
* Description: User input from `username` parameter is directly interpolated into HTML without escaping, allowing reflected XSS attacks
* Exploit Scenario: Attacker crafts URL like /bar?q=<script>alert(document.cookie)</script> to execute JavaScript in victim's browser, enabling session hijacking or data theft
* Recommendation: Use Flask's escape() function or Jinja2 templates with auto-escaping enabled for all user inputs rendered in HTML

SEVERITY GUIDELINES:
- **HIGH**: Directly exploitable vulnerabilities leading to RCE, data breach, or authentication bypass
- **MEDIUM**: Vulnerabilities requiring specific conditions but with significant impact
- **LOW**: Defense-in-depth issues or lower-impact vulnerabilities

CONFIDENCE SCORING:
- 0.9-1.0: Certain exploit path identified, tested if possible
- 0.8-0.9: Clear vulnerability pattern with known exploitation methods
- 0.7-0.8: Suspicious pattern requiring specific conditions to exploit
- Below 0.7: Don't report (too speculative)

FINAL REMINDER:
Focus on HIGH and MEDIUM findings only. Better to miss some theoretical issues than flood the report with false positives. Each finding should be something a security engineer would confidently raise in a PR review.

FALSE POSITIVE FILTERING:

> You do not need to run commands to reproduce the vulnerability, just read the code to determine if it is a real vulnerability. Do not use the bash tool or write to any files.
>
> HARD EXCLUSIONS - Automatically exclude findings matching these patterns:
> 1. Denial of Service (DOS) vulnerabilities or resource exhaustion attacks.
> 2. Secrets or credentials stored on disk if they are otherwise secured.
> 3. Rate limiting concerns or service overload scenarios.
> 4. Memory consumption or CPU exhaustion issues.
> 5. Lack of input validation on non-security-critical fields without proven security impact.
> 6. Input sanitization concerns for GitHub Action workflows unless they are clearly triggerable via untrusted input.
> 7. A lack of hardening measures. Code is not expected to implement all security best practices, only flag concrete vulnerabilities.
> 8. Race conditions or timing attacks that are theoretical rather than practical issues. Only report a race condition if it is concretely problematic.
> 9. Vulnerabilities related to outdated third-party libraries. These are managed separately and should not be reported here.
> 10. Memory safety issues such as buffer overflows or use-after-free-vulnerabilities are impossible in rust. Do not report memory safety issues in rust or any other memory safe languages.
> 11. Files that are only unit tests or only used as part of running tests.
> 12. Log spoofing concerns. Outputting un-sanitized user input to logs is not a vulnerability.
> 13. SSRF vulnerabilities that only control the path. SSRF is only a concern if it can control the host or protocol.
> 14. Including user-controlled content in AI system prompts is not a vulnerability.
> 15. Regex injection. Injecting untrusted content into a regex is not a vulnerability.
> 16. Regex DOS concerns.
> 16. Insecure documentation. Do not report any findings in documentation files such as markdown files.
> 17. A lack of audit logs is not a vulnerability.
>
> PRECEDENTS -
> 1. Logging high value secrets in plaintext is a vulnerability. Logging URLs is assumed to be safe.
> 2. UUIDs can be assumed to be unguessable and do not need to be validated.
> 3. Environment variables and CLI flags are trusted values. Attackers are generally not able to modify them in a secure environment. Any attack that relies on controlling an environment variable is invalid.
> 4. Resource management issues such as memory or file descriptor leaks are not valid.
> 5. Subtle or low impact web vulnerabilities such as tabnabbing, XS-Leaks, prototype pollution, and open redirects should not be reported unless they are extremely high confidence.
> 6. React and Angular are generally secure against XSS. These frameworks do not need to sanitize or escape user input unless it is using dangerouslySetInnerHTML, bypassSecurityTrustHtml, or similar methods. Do not report XSS vulnerabilities in React or Angular components or tsx files unless they are using unsafe methods.
> 7. Most vulnerabilities in github action workflows are not exploitable in practice. Before validating a github action workflow vulnerability ensure it is concrete and has a very specific attack path.
> 8. A lack of permission checking or authentication in client-side JS/TS code is not a vulnerability. Client-side code is not trusted and does not need to implement these checks, they are handled on the server-side. The same applies to all flows that send untrusted data to the backend, the backend is responsible for validating and sanitizing all inputs.
> 9. Only include MEDIUM findings if they are obvious and concrete issues.
> 10. Most vulnerabilities in ipython notebooks (*.ipynb files) are not exploitable in practice. Before validating a notebook vulnerability ensure it is concrete and has a very specific attack path where untrusted input can trigger the vulnerability.
> 11. Logging non-PII data is not a vulnerability even if the data may be sensitive. Only report logging vulnerabilities if they expose sensitive information such as secrets, passwords, or personally identifiable information (PII).
> 12. Command injection vulnerabilities in shell scripts are generally not exploitable in practice since shell scripts generally do not run with untrusted user input. Only report command injection vulnerabilities in shell scripts if they are concrete and have a very specific attack path for untrusted input.
>
> SIGNAL QUALITY CRITERIA - For remaining findings, assess:
> 1. Is there a concrete, exploitable vulnerability with a clear attack path?
> 2. Does this represent a real security risk vs theoretical best practice?
> 3. Are there specific code locations and reproduction steps?
> 4. Would this finding be actionable for a security team?
>
> For each finding, assign a confidence score from 1-10:
> - 1-3: Low confidence, likely false positive or noise
> - 4-6: Medium confidence, needs investigation
> - 7-10: High confidence, likely true vulnerability

START ANALYSIS:

Begin your analysis now. Do this in 3 steps:

1. Use a sub-task to identify vulnerabilities. Use the repository exploration tools to understand the codebase context, then analyze the PR changes for security implications. In the prompt for this sub-task, include all of the above.
2. Then for each vulnerability identified by the above sub-task, create a new sub-task to filter out false-positives. Launch these sub-tasks as parallel sub-tasks. In the prompt for these sub-tasks, include everything in the "FALSE POSITIVE FILTERING" instructions.
3. Filter out any vulnerabilities where the sub-task reported a confidence less than 8.

Your final reply must contain the markdown report and nothing else.

Based on the sub-task analysis (identification + false-positive assessment), here is the security review report.

# Security Review — vision-mcp Fatia 2 (uncommitted changes)

## Vuln 1: SSRF (redirect-follow host bypass): `packages/vision-mcp-core/src/tools/analyze-video.ts:104-112` (`fetchInlineVideo`)

* **Severity:** MEDIUM
* **Confidence:** 8/10
* **Category:** `ssrf` (host/protocol-controlling — in scope)
* **Description:** `assertSafeVideoUrl()` (analyze-video.ts:50-73) validates only the **initial** URL's hostname against the private/loopback/link-local denylist and enforces `https:`. The subsequent `fetchInlineVideo()` calls bare `fetch(url)`, and Node/undici `fetch` follows 3xx redirects automatically (`redirect:"follow"`, ≤20 hops) **without re-validating the redirect target** — and a `302 Location: http://169.254.169.254/...` downgrades past the `https`-only gate too. The container holds `SB_SECRET_KEY` and sits on the internal Docker mesh network (chroma/claude_mem/daemon) on cloud infra, so this is a real internal-network reachability gain, not a path-only SSRF.
* **Exploit Scenario:** Any authenticated tenant (free if they hold a BYOK `google_api_key` → cost 0; otherwise 2 mco, refunded on the downstream failure) calls `vision_analyze_video` with `video_url=https://attacker.com/v.mp4`. The attacker host returns `302 → http://169.254.169.254/opc/v1/instance/` (OCI IMDS) or an internal mesh service (`http://mcorch_chroma:8001/...`). The container fetches the internal/metadata target, base64-encodes the body, and sends it to Gemini; Gemini's natural-language description of those bytes is returned to the caller — a low-bandwidth read-exfil of internal responses plus blind GET reachability to any internal service. The `describe_image` path is NOT affected (there OpenRouter fetches the image server-side; the container does not).
* **Recommendation:** Disable auto-redirect and re-validate every hop: call `fetch(url, { redirect: "manual" })`, and on a 3xx re-run `assertSafeVideoUrl` (incl. the `https:`-only check) against the resolved `Location` before re-fetching, with a hop cap. Better still, resolve the host to its IP(s) and reject any private/link-local/loopback address (also defeats DNS-rebinding). Apply to any path where the container itself fetches a user-supplied URL.

---

## Cleared (no finding)
- **Migration `20260615140000` (Firecrawl column):** faithful additive replication of the live `20260603240000` template — no existing encryption block altered; INSTEAD OF tenant guard (`auth.uid()` → `42501`) preserved verbatim; `decrypted_user_api_keys` keeps `REVOKE ALL … FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` + `GRANT SELECT … TO service_role`; masked view keeps row filter + `••••` mask; `search_path=''` preserved. No secret leaked.
- **mcoCoins billing:** `deductOnEntry`/`refund` call service-role RPCs with the verified `sub`; `add_mco_coins` is service-role-only, `deduct_mco_coins` self-guarded; refund amount always equals the just-succeeded deduct; `cost` derives from sealed constants × clamped `billedMinutes`∈[1,30]. No mint/drain.
- **Identity/tenancy:** ES256 fail-closed verify, `sub` trimmed; all tools scope by the verified `sub`, never tool input. `consolidate_reference` writes `user_id: sub`; credential lookup `user_id=eq.<sub>`.
- **Injection:** tool args go as parameterized JSON bodies; `node_type` allowlisted. No SQL/PostgREST injection.
- **`isProtocolVersionOk` broadening / docker-compose env / `src/lib/billing.ts`:** interop negotiation (not auth); server-side-only env keys; cost constants only. No regression.

```json
  "old_string": "async function fetchInlineVideo(url: string): Promise<{ mimeType: string; data: string }> {\n  const res = await fetch(url);\n  if (!res.ok) throw new VideoUrlError(`video_fetch_${res.status}`);\n  const buf = Buffer.from(await res.arrayBuffer());\n  if (buf.length > MAX_VIDEO_BYTES) throw new VideoUrlError(\"video_too_large\");\n  const ct = (res.headers.get(\"content-type\") ?? \"video/mp4\").split(\";\")[0].trim() || \"video/mp4\";\n  return { mimeType: ct, data: buf.toString(\"base64\") };\n}",
  "new_string": "// Redirect-safe fetch: the container itself downloads this URL, so EVERY hop must be re-validated\n// against the SSRF guard (a public host could 302 \u2192 http://169.254.169.254 / an internal mesh service,\n// and default fetch follows redirects + downgrades to http). We follow manually and re-assert each hop.\nasync function fetchInlineVideo(url: string): Promise<{ mimeType: string; data: string }> {\n  let current = url;\n  let res: Response | undefined;\n  for (let hop = 0; hop < 5; hop++) {\n    assertSafeVideoUrl(current); // re-validate https-only + private/loopback denylist on EVERY hop\n    res = await fetch(current, { redirect: \"manual\" });\n    if (res.status >= 300 && res.status < 400) {\n      const loc = res.headers.get(\"location\");\n      if (!loc) throw new VideoUrlError(\"video_fetch_bad_redirect\");\n      current = new URL(loc, current).toString();\n      continue;\n    }\n    break;\n  }\n  if (!res) throw new VideoUrlError(\"video_fetch_too_many_redirects\");\n  if (!res.ok) throw new VideoUrlError(`video_fetch_${res.status}`);\n  const buf = Buffer.from(await res.arrayBuffer());\n  if (buf.length > MAX_VIDEO_BYTES) throw new VideoUrlError(\"video_too_large\");\n  const ct = (res.headers.get(\"content-type\") ?? \"video/mp4\").split(\";\")[0].trim() || \"video/mp4\";\n  return { mimeType: ct, data: buf.toString(\"base64\") };\n}"
}
```

```json
  "content": "// vision-mcp-core tools/deepsearch-scrape.ts \u2014 FR-VM-007 (deepsearch.scrape, 1 mco). Scrapes a public\n// URL \u2192 clean markdown via Firecrawl. BYOK-ONLY (Sovereign directive 2026-06-15): the per-user\n// firecrawl_api_key is resolved (BYOK \u21d2 free; absent \u21d2 402 firecrawl_not_configured \u2014 no platform fallback).\n// NOTE: Firecrawl fetches the target URL from ITS OWN infra, so this is not a container-side SSRF surface\n// (unlike analyze_video's inline fetch); we still require https + a parseable URL.\n\nimport { resolveProviderKey } from \"../auth/credentials.js\";\nimport { costFor, deductOnEntry, refund, ProviderNotConfiguredError } from \"../infra/billing.js\";\nimport { logHealth } from \"../infra/telemetry.js\";\n\nconst FIRECRAWL_API = process.env.FIRECRAWL_API_URL ?? \"https://api.firecrawl.dev/v1/scrape\";\nconst MAX_MARKDOWN_CHARS = 100_000;\n\nexport interface DeepsearchScrapeInput {\n  url: string;\n}\nexport interface DeepsearchScrapeResult {\n  url: string;\n  title?: string;\n  markdown: string;\n  cost_mco: number;\n}\n\nexport class ScrapeUrlError extends Error {\n  code: string;\n  constructor(code: string) {\n    super(code);\n    this.code = code;\n  }\n}\n\nexport function assertHttpsUrl(raw: string): URL {\n  let u: URL;\n  try {\n    u = new URL(raw);\n  } catch {\n    throw new ScrapeUrlError(\"invalid_url\");\n  }\n  if (u.protocol !== \"https:\") throw new ScrapeUrlError(\"url_must_be_https\");\n  return u;\n}\n\nexport async function deepsearchScrape(input: DeepsearchScrapeInput, sub: string): Promise<DeepsearchScrapeResult> {\n  const u = assertHttpsUrl(input.url);\n\n  // BYOK-only resolution (no platform key) \u21d2 free for BYOK, 402 otherwise.\n  const resolved = await resolveProviderKey(sub, \"firecrawl\");\n  if (!resolved) throw new ProviderNotConfiguredError(\"firecrawl\");\n\n  const cost = costFor(\"deepsearch.scrape\", sub, resolved.isUserCustomKey);\n  await deductOnEntry(sub, cost, \"deepsearch.scrape\", { url_host: u.hostname });\n\n  try {\n    const res = await fetch(FIRECRAWL_API, {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${resolved.key}`, \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n      body: JSON.stringify({ url: input.url, formats: [\"markdown\"], onlyMainContent: true }),\n    });\n    if (!res.ok) {\n      throw new Error(`firecrawl_http_${res.status}:${(await res.text()).slice(0, 160)}`);\n    }\n    const data = (await res.json()) as {\n      success?: boolean;\n      data?: { markdown?: string; metadata?: { title?: string } };\n    };\n    if (!data.success || !data.data) throw new Error(\"firecrawl_no_data\");\n    const markdown = (data.data.markdown ?? \"\").slice(0, MAX_MARKDOWN_CHARS);\n    logHealth(\"healthy\", \"tool_ok\", { tool: \"deepsearch_scrape\", cost, byok: resolved.isUserCustomKey });\n    return { url: input.url, title: data.data.metadata?.title, markdown, cost_mco: cost };\n  } catch (e) {\n    await refund(sub, cost, \"deepsearch_scrape_failed\");\n    throw e;\n  }\n}\n"
}
```

process.env.VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY ?? \"\";\nconst MCP = process.env.VISION_MCP_URL ?? \"http://127.0.0.1:3200/mcp\";\nconst BYOK = process.env.FIRECRAWL_BYOK_KEY ?? \"\";\nif (!SB_URL || !SVC || !PUB) { console.error(\"Missing env\"); process.exit(2); }\nconst admin: SupabaseClient = createClient(SB_URL, SVC, { auth: { persistSession: false } });\nconst anon: SupabaseClient = createClient(SB_URL, PUB, { auth: { persistSession: false } });\n\ntype S = { id: string; pass: boolean; ev: string };\nconst steps: S[] = [];\nfunction rec(id: string, pass: boolean, ev: string) { steps.push({ id, pass, ev }); console.log(`${pass ? \"\u2705\" : \"\u274c\"} ${id} \u2014 ${ev}`); }\n\nlet userId = \"\", jwt = \"\";\nasync function balance(): Promise<number> {\n  const r = await fetch(`${SB_URL}/rest/v1/profiles?id=eq.${userId}&select=mco_balance`, { headers: { apikey: SVC, Authorization: `Bearer ${SVC}` } });\n  return (await r.json())?.[0]?.mco_balance ?? -1;\n}\nasync function call(args: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<{ isError: boolean; result: any }> {\n  const transport = new StreamableHTTPClientTransport(new URL(MCP), { requestInit: { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${jwt}` } } });\n  const client = new Client({ name: \"smoke\", version: \"1.0.0\" }, { capabilities: {} });\n  await client.connect(transport);\n  try {\n    const res: any = await client.callTool({ name: \"deepsearch_scrape\", arguments: args });\n    const text = res.content?.[0]?.type === \"text\" ? res.content[0].text : \"\";\n    let parsed = res.structuredContent ?? (text ? safeParse(text) : null);\n    return { isError: res.isError === true, result: parsed ?? { rawText: text } };\n  } finally { await client.close(); }\n}\nfunction safeParse(s: string) { try { return JSON.parse(s); } catch { return null; } }\n\nasync function main() {\n  console.log(`\\n\u2550\u2550 Vision MCP deepsearch_scrape smoke \u2550\u2550 ${MCP}\\n`);\n  const email = `qa+dss${Date.now().toString(36)}@vm.local`;\n  const { data: u, error: ue } = await admin.auth.admin.createUser({ email, email_confirm: true });\n  if (ue || !u.user) throw new Error(`createUser: ${ue?.message}`);\n  userId = u.user.id;\n  const { data: l } = await admin.auth.admin.generateLink({ type: \"magiclink\", email });\n  for (const t of [\"magiclink\", \"email\"] as const) { const { data: s, error } = await anon.auth.verifyOtp({ token_hash: l!.properties!.hashed_token, type: t }); if (!error && s?.session) { jwt = s.session.access_token; break; } }\n  if (!jwt) throw new Error(\"verifyOtp failed\");\n  await fetch(`${SB_URL}/rest/v1/profiles?id=eq.${userId}`, { method: \"PATCH\", headers: { apikey: SVC, Authorization: `Bearer ${SVC}`, \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" }, body: JSON.stringify({ mco_balance: 0 }) });\n  console.log(`   throwaway user=${userId}  (balance 0; proves BYOK is free)\\n`);\n\n  // D1 \u2014 tools/list\n  {\n    const transport = new StreamableHTTPClientTransport(new URL(MCP), { requestInit: { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${jwt}` } } });\n    const client = new Client({ name: \"smoke\", version: \"1.0.0\" }, { capabilities: {} });\n    await client.connect(transport);\n    const tools = (await client.listTools()).tools.map((t: any) => t.name);\n    await client.close();\n    rec(\"D1\", tools.includes(\"deepsearch_scrape\"), `tools=${JSON.stringify(tools)}`);\n  }\n  // D2 \u2014 url guard (http)\n  { const r = await call({ url: \"http://example.com\" }); rec(\"D2\", r.isError && /https/.test(JSON.stringify(r.result)), JSON.stringify(r.result).slice(0, 80)); }\n  // D3 \u2014 not configured (no BYOK, no platform key)\n  { const r = await call({ url: \"https://example.com\" }); rec(\"D3\", r.isError && /firecrawl_not_configured/.test(JSON.stringify(r.result)), JSON.stringify(r.result).slice(0, 90)); }\n\n  // set the per-user firecrawl BYOK key via the encrypted view (service-role \u2192 INSTEAD OF \u2192 Vault)\n  if (!BYOK) { rec(\"D4\", false, \"FIRECRAWL_BYOK_KEY not provided \u2014 cannot prove the real BYOK leg\"); return; }\n  const ins = await admin.from(\"user_api_keys\").insert({ user_id: userId, firecrawl_api_key: BYOK } as any);\n  if (ins.error) { rec(\"D4\", false, `BYOK insert failed: ${ins.error.message}`); return; }\n  // confirm the decrypted view returns a non-empty key for this user (no value printed)\n  const dec = await fetch(`${SB_URL}/rest/v1/decrypted_user_api_keys?user_id=eq.${userId}&select=firecrawl_api_key`, { headers: { apikey: SVC, Authorization: `Bearer ${SVC}` } });\n  const decKeyLen = ((await dec.json())?.[0]?.firecrawl_api_key ?? \"\").length;\n  console.log(`   BYOK set; decrypted firecrawl key len=${decKeyLen}`);\n\n  // D4 \u2014 real BYOK scrape (free): expect markdown + cost_mco 0 + balance unchanged (0)\n  {\n    const before = await balance();\n    const r = await call({ url: \"https://example.com\" });\n    const after = await balance();\n    const md = r.result?.markdown ?? \"\";\n    const ok = !r.isError && typeof md === \"string\" && md.length > 10 && r.result?.cost_mco === 0 && after === before;\n    rec(\"D4\", ok, `cost_mco=${r.result?.cost_mco} title=\"${r.result?.title ?? \"\"}\" md_len=${md.length} balance ${before}\u2192${after} md_head=\"${String(md).replace(/\\n/g, \" \").slice(0, 60)}\"`);\n  }\n}\n\nmain()\n  .catch((e) => { console.error(\"\\n\ud83d\udca5\", e); rec(\"FATAL\", false, String(e)); })\n  .finally(async () => {\n    if (userId) { const { error } = await admin.auth.admin.deleteUser(userId); console.log(`\\n  cleanup user ${userId}: ${error ? \"ERR \" + error.message : \"deleted\"}`); }\n    const p = steps.filter((s) => s.pass).length;\n    console.log(`\\n\u2550\u2550 RESULT: ${p}/${steps.length} steps passed \u2550\u2550`);\n    process.exit(steps.every((s) => s.pass) ? 0 : 1);\n  });\n"
```json
}
```
